As a consequence,
there exists
K_{3}>0
, that depends only on
d
,
n
,
C_{0}
, and
C_{1}
, such that
for
\beta\geq 20
,

(11.139)

u_{\beta}(X)\leq K_{3}\beta^{-\alpha}u_{4}(X)\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\cap[B(y_{0},10s)\setminus B(y_{0},8s)].

We just proved that for
\beta\geq 20
,
the function
u^{\prime}=K_{3}\beta^{-\alpha}u_{4}-u_{\beta}
satisfies the assumption (iii) of
Lemma
11.3
, with

E=B(y_{0},10s)
and
F=\overline{B(y_{0},8s)}
.
The other assumptions of Lemma
11.3
are satisfied as well, since
u^{\prime}\in W
is smooth and
T(u^{\prime})=K_{3}\beta^{-\alpha}Tu_{4}\geq 0
on
\Gamma\cap E
.
Therefore,
Lemma
11.3
gives

(11.140)

u_{\beta}(X)\leq K_{3}\beta^{-\alpha}u_{4}(X)\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\cap B(y_{0},10s).

Use now (
11.132
) and Lemma
11.11
to get for

X\in\Omega\cap[B(y_{0},s)\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4)]
,

(11.141)

u_{\beta}(X)\leq K_{3}\beta^{-\alpha}\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus B(y_{0},2s))\leq C\beta^{-\alpha}s^{d-1}g(X,Y_{0}),

where
C>0
depends only on
d
,
n
,
C_{0}
and
C_{1}
.
The existence of
K_{2}\geq 20
satisfying (
11.136
) is now immediate.

Define the function
f_{y_{0},s}
on
\Omega\setminus\{Y_{0}\}
by

(11.142)

f_{y_{0},s}(X):=s^{d-1}g(X,Y_{0})-K_{1}u_{K_{2}}(X).

The inequality (
11.135
) gives

(11.143)

f_{y_{0},s}(X)\leq 0\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\setminus B(y_{0},2K_{2}s),

and the estimates (
11.136
) and (
11.69
) imply that

(11.144)

f_{y_{0},s}(X)\geq\frac{1}{2}s^{d-1}g(X,Y_{0})\geq C^{-1}\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus B(y_{0},2s))\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\cap[B(y_{0},s)\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4)].

Let us turn to the proof of the comparison principle.
By symmetry and as
in Lemma
11.14
, it suffices to prove the upper bound in (
11.130
), that is

(11.145)

\frac{u(X)}{v(X)}\leq C\frac{u(X_{0})}{v(X_{0})}\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\cap B(x_{0},r).

We claim that

(11.146)

v(X)\geq C^{-1}v(X_{0})\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus B(x_{0},2r))\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\cap B(x_{0},r),

where
C>0
depends only on
n
,
d
,
C_{0}
and
C_{1}
.
So let
X\in\Omega\cap B(x_{0},r)
be given. Two cases may happen.
If
\delta(X)\geq\frac{r}{8K_{2}}
, where
K_{2}
comes from (
11.136
)
and is the same as in the definition of
f_{y_{0},s}
, the
existence of Harnack chains (Lemma
2.2
), the Harnack inequality (Lemma
8.9
) and the non-degeneracy of the harmonic measure (Lemma
11.10
) give

(11.147)

v(X)\approx v(X_{0})\approx v(X_{0})\frac{\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus B(x_{0},2r))}{\omega^{X_{0}}(\Gamma\setminus B(x_{0},2r))}\approx v(X_{0})\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus B(x_{0},2r))

by
(
11.67
).
The more interesting remaining case is when

\delta(X)<\frac{r}{8K_{2}}
.
Take
y_{0}\in\Gamma
such that
|X-y_{0}|=\delta(X)
. Set

s:=\frac{r}{8K_{2}}
and
Y_{0}=A_{s}(y_{0})
.
The ball
B(y_{0},\frac{1}{2}r)=B(y_{0},4K_{2}s)
is contained
in
B(x_{0},\frac{7}{4}r)
. The following points hold :

•

The quantity
\int_{B(y_{0},4K_{2}s)\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4)}|\nabla v|^{2}dm
is finite
because
v\in W_{r}(B(x_{0},2r))
. The fact that

\int_{B(y_{0},4K_{2}s)\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4)}|\nabla f_{y_{0},s}|^{2}dm
is finite as well
follows from the property (
10.2
) of the Green function.

•

There exists
K_{4}>0
(depending only on
d
,
n
,
C_{0}
and
C_{1}
) such that

(11.148)

v(Y)-K_{4}v(Y_{0})f_{y_{0},s}(Y)\geq 0\ \ \ \text{ for }Y\in B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/2)\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4).

This latter inequality is due to the following two bounds: the fact that

(11.149)

f_{y_{0},s}(Y)\leq s^{1-d}g(Y,Y_{0})\leq C\ \ \ \text{ for }Y\in B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/2)\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4),

which is a consequence of
the definition (
11.142
) and
(
10.6
), and the bound

(11.150)

v(Y)\geq C^{-1}v(Y_{0})\ \ \ \text{ for }Y\in B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/2),

which comes from the Harnack inequality (Lemma
8.9
).

•

The function
v-K_{4}v(Y_{0})f_{y_{0},s}

is nonnegative on

\Omega\cap[B(y_{0},4K_{2}s)\setminus B(y_{0},2K_{2}s)]
. Indeed,
v\geq 0
on
B(y_{0},4K_{2}s)
and, thanks to (
11.143
),
f_{y_{0},s}\leq 0
on
\Omega\setminus B(y_{0},2K_{2}s)
.

•

The trace of
v-K_{4}v(Y_{0})f_{y_{0},s}
is non-negative on

B(y_{0},4K_{2}s)\cap\Gamma

again because
Tv=0
on

B(y_{0},4K_{2}s)\cap\Gamma

and
T[f_{y_{0},s}]\leq 0
on

B(y_{0},4K_{2}s)\cap\Gamma

by construction.

The previous points prove that
v-K_{4}v(Y_{0})f_{y_{0},s}
satisfies the assumptions of
Lemma
11.3
with

E=B(y_{0},4K_{2}s)\setminus\overline{B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4)}
and
F=\overline{B(y_{0},2K_{2}s)}\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/2)
. As a consequence, for any
Y\in B(y_{0},4K_{2}s)\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4)

(11.151)

v(Y)-K_{4}v(Y_{0})f_{y_{0},s}(Y)\geq 0,

and hence,
for any
Y\in B(y_{0},s)\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4)

(11.152)

v(Y)\geq K_{4}v(Y_{0})f_{y_{0},s}(Y)\geq C^{-1}v(Y_{0})\omega^{Y}(\Gamma\setminus B(y_{0},2s))

by
(
11.144
). Since
both
v
and
Y\to\omega^{Y}(\Gamma\setminus B(y_{0},2s))
are solutions on
B(y_{0},2s)
,
we can use the Harnack inequality (Lemma
8.9
) to deduce, first,
that (
11.152
) holds for any
Y\in B(y_{0},s)
and second, that we can replace
v(Y_{0})
by
v(X_{0})

(recall that at this point,
\frac{s}{r}=\frac{1}{8K_{2}}
is controlled by the usual constants).
Therefore,

(11.153)

v(Y)\geq C^{-1}v(X_{0})\omega^{Y}(\Gamma\setminus B(y_{0},2s))\ \ \ \text{ for }Y\in B(y_{0},s).

In particular, with our choice of
y_{0}
and
s
, the inequality is true when
X=Y
, that is,

(11.154)

v(X)\geq C^{-1}v(X_{0})\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus B(y_{0},2s))\geq C^{-1}v(X_{0})\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus B(x_{0},2r))

where
C>0
depends only on
d
,
n
,
C_{0}
and
C_{1}
.
The claim (
11.146
) follows.

Now we want to prove that

(11.155)

u(X)\leq Cu(X_{0})\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus B(x_{0},\frac{5}{4}r))\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\cap B(x_{0},r).

By
Lemma
11.8
,

(11.156)

u(X)\leq Cu(X_{0})\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\cap B(x_{0},\frac{7}{4}r).

Pick

h^{\prime}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
such that
0\leq h^{\prime}\leq 1
,
h^{\prime}\equiv 1
outside of
B(x_{0},\frac{3}{2}r)
,
and
h^{\prime}\equiv 0
on
B(x_{0},\frac{5}{4}r)
.
Let
u_{h^{\prime}}=U(h^{\prime})
be
the solution of
Lu_{h^{\prime}}=0
with the data
Tu_{h^{\prime}}=Th^{\prime}
(given by Lemma
9.1
).
As before,
u_{h^{\prime}}\in W
because
1-u_{h^{\prime}}=U(1-h)
and
1-h
is a test function.
Also,
u_{h^{\prime}}(X)\geq\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus B(x_{0},\frac{3}{2}r))
by monotonicity.
So (
11.66
), which
states the non-degeneracy of the harmonic measure, gives

(11.157)

u_{h^{\prime}}(X)\geq C^{-1}\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\setminus B(x_{0},\frac{13}{8}r).

The combination of (
11.156
) and (
11.157
) yields the existence of
K_{5}>0
(that depends
only on
d
,
n
,
C_{0}
and
C_{1}
) such that
K_{5}u(X_{0})u_{h^{\prime}}-u\geq 0
on
\Omega\cap[B(x_{0},\frac{7}{4}r)\setminus B(x_{0},\frac{13}{8}r)]
.
It is easy to check that
K_{5}u(X_{0})u_{h^{\prime}}-u
satisfies all the assumptions of Lemma
11.3
, with

E=B(x_{0},\frac{7}{4}r)
and
F=\overline{B(x_{0},\frac{13}{8}r)}
.
This is because
u\in W_{r}(B(x_{0},2r))
,
u_{h^{\prime}}\in W
,
Tu_{h^{\prime}}\geq 0
, and
Tu=0
on

\Gamma\cap B(x_{0},2r)
.
Then by Lemma
11.3

(11.158)

u\leq K_{5}u(X_{0})u_{h^{\prime}}\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\cap B(x_{0},\frac{7}{4}r),

and since
u_{h^{\prime}}(X)\leq\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus B(x_{0},\frac{5}{4}r))

for all

X\in\Omega
,

(11.159)

u(X)\leq Cu(X_{0})\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus B(x_{0},\frac{5}{4}r))\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\cap B(x_{0},\frac{7}{4}r).

The claim (
11.155
) follows.

The bounds (
11.146
) and (
11.155
) imply that

(11.160)

\frac{u(X)}{v(X)}\leq C\frac{u(X_{0})}{v(X_{0})}\frac{\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus B(x_{0},\frac{5}{4}r))}{\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus B(x_{0},2r))}\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\cap B(x_{0},r).

The
bound (
11.145
) is now
a consequence of the above inequality and the doubling property of the harmonic measure (Lemma
11.12
, or more exactly (
11.100
)).

\Box

Index
